    def test_decoder_accuracy(metrics, submitted_data_full, submitted_data_stats, reference_data_stats):
        """Run the provided train_decoder.py script on the sample data and check that it runs without error."""
    
        train_params = {
            'npcs': 100,
            'lr': 1e-3,
            'l1_weight': 1e-4,
            'balanced_loss': True,
        }
    
        metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy'] = None
    
        if reference_data_stats is not None:
            ref_accuracy = reference_data_stats['validation_balanced_accuracy']
            metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy_reference'] = ref_accuracy
            metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy_ratio'] = None
    
        # train the decoder on the submitted data
        rng_state = reference_data_stats.get("rng_state") if reference_data_stats else None
        try:
            scores, predictions, pcs, confidences, train_idx, test_idx, model, output, _ = \
                train_validate_decoder(submitted_data_full['neural'], submitted_data_full['input'],
                                    submitted_data_full['output'], state=rng_state, **train_params)

            print("CUDA out of memory error during decoder training. Retrying on CPU...")
            train_params['device'] = torch.device('cpu')

Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n   270→\n   271→i. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward event timestamps are matched to behavior timestamps, and a binary `isreward` array marks timepoints where rewards occurred.\n   272→\n   273→ii.\n   274→```python\n   275→reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\n   276→reward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\n   277→reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n   278→isreward = np.zeros(T)\n   279→isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n   280→```\n   281→\n   282→iii. The `Reward` time series has its own timestamps (not aligned to the behavior sampling rate), so `searchsorted` is used to find the nearest timepoint. An assertion checks that the alignment error is within half a time bin.\n   283→\n   284→## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n   285→\n   286→i. For each trial, check whether any reward event occurred in the *previous* trial's time range. For the first trial, set to 0. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n   287→\n   288→ii.\n   289→```python\n   290→if trial == 0:\n   291→    input_curr[3,:] = 0\n   292→else:\n   293→    prev_reward_idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial-1], trial_end_idx[trial-1], dtype=int)\n   294→    isprevreward = np.any(isreward[prev_reward_idx])\n   295→    input_curr[3,:] = int(isprevreward)\n   296→```\n   297→\n   298→iii. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n   299→\n   300→## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n   301→\n   302→i. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n   303→\n   304→ii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n   305→- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n   306→```python\n   307→behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n   308→info['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\n   309→position = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\n   310→for trial in range(ntrials):\n   311→    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   312→    pos_trial = position[idx]\n   313→    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n   314→    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n   315→        continue\n   316→    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n   317→```\n   318→The function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n   319→```python\n   320→reward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n   321→                    'B': [200, 250],\n   322→                    'C': [320, 370]}\n   323→...\n   324→sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n   325→zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n   326→dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n   327→                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n   328→dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n   329→log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n   330→```\n   331→It also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n   332→```python\n   333→pstay = .9\n   334→log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n   335→np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n   336→```\n   337→\n   338→iii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n   339→\n   340→## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n   341→\n   342→i. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.\n   343→\n   344→ii.\n   345→```python\n   346→def compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n   347→    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n   348→    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n   349→    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n   350→    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n   351→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n   352→    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n   353→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n   354→    return distance_to_reward_zone\n   355→```\n   356→\n   357→iii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`.\n   358→\n   359→## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n   360→\n   361→i. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n   362→\n   363→ii.\n   364→```python\n   365→distance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n   366→...\n   367→output_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n   368→```\n   369→\n   370→iii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n   371→\n   372→## 7-d. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nif trial == 0:\n    input_curr[3,:] = 0\nelse:\n    prev_reward_idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial-1], trial_end_idx[trial-1], dtype=int)\n    isprevreward = np.any(isreward[prev_reward_idx])\n    input_curr[3,:] = int(isprevreward)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n```python\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \ninfo['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    pos_trial = position[idx]\n    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n        continue\n    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n```\nThe function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n...\nsigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n```\nIt also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n```python\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n```\n\niii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The position data and neural data share the same time indices within each trial, so no additional alignment is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\nidx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\nspike_data_curr = spike_data[idx]\nposition_curr = position[idx]\n```\n\niii. Both neural and behavioral data use the same time indexing (verified by timestamp assertions earlier in the code), so indexing by the same `idx` array ensures alignment.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:][:T]\nassert np.allclose(behavior_data['position'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for position do not match trial number timestamps\"\n...\nposition_curr = position[idx]\n```\n\niii. The `position` variable directly records the animal's position in the VR corridor in cm.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. No processing beyond extracting the per-trial slice and discretizing.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition_curr = position[idx]\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The raw position values are used directly.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 5 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, 50, 150, 250, 350, inf]`, corresponding to 100 cm wide bins spanning the corridor. The position ranged from -50 to 450. \n\nii.\n```python\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,50,150,250,350,np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify \"5 equal-sized bins\". The corridor spans roughly -50 to 450 cm (from survey), so 100 cm bins are approximately equal-sized.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as the neural data within each trial — no additional alignment needed.\n\nii. Same indexing as neural: `position_curr = position[idx]`\n\niii. Verified by timestamp assertions.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `lick` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nlicks = behavior_data['lick'].data[:][:T]\nassert np.allclose(behavior_data['lick'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for lick do not match trial number timestamps\"\n...\nlicks_curr = licks[idx]\n```\n\niii. The `lick` variable records lick events at each timepoint.\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Binarized: any positive lick value is mapped to 1, otherwise 0.\n\nii.\n```python\noutput_curr[3] = (licks_curr > 0).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify binary output (no/yes). The raw lick values can be >1, so thresholding at >0 converts to binary.\n\n## 9-c. How is `output` *Lick* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as the neural data within each trial — no additional alignment needed.\n\nii. Same indexing as neural: `licks_curr = licks[idx]`\n\niii. Verified by timestamp assertions.\n\n## 10-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward zone location* derived from?\n\ni. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward events have their own timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n...\noutput_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The `Reward` time series records reward delivery events with separate timestamps from the behavior sampling rate.\n\n## 11-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward outcome*?\n\ni. Reward event timestamps are mapped to the nearest behavior timepoint using `searchsorted`. For each trial, the output is 1 if any reward event occurred within the trial, 0 otherwise. The value is constant across all timepoints in the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nerr = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\nassert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n...\noutput_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The alignment error is checked to be within half a time bin. `np.any` is used because a trial either was rewarded or not — the specific timepoint doesn't matter for the per-trial binary output.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The per-trial loop is the natural structure given variable-length trials. Vectorizing would require padding or masking.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. I don't think these could be run at the same time, as it would require too much RAM. The Viterbi output created during surveying is required to process the data. It was convenient to run the survey before deciding how to handle the conversion of the data. \n\n## 13-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. in the NWB file\n- A trial is \"rewarded\" if any reward timestamp falls within the trial boundaries\n- Previous trial outcome for the first trial of each session is set to 0\n\n## Reward Zone Determination\n\nReward zone locations per trial are determined from the session scene metadata (from `sessions_dict.py`):\n- **Zone A**: 80-130 cm\n- **Zone B**: 200-250 cm\n- **Zone C**: 320-370 cm\n\nOn switch days, trials 0-29 use the pre-switch zone and trials 30+ use the post-switch zone (per paper: \"On day 3, the reward zone was moved after 30 trials\").\n\n## Environment Type\n\nDetermined from scene name:\n- Sessions with \"Env1\" only: environment = 0\n- Sessions with \"Env2\" only: environment = 1\n- Day 8 sessions (environment switch): first 30 trials = original env, remaining = new env\n\n## Temporal Alignment\n\n- Aligned to **start of trial** (trial_start event)\n- Time bin size: ~64.48 ms (1/15.5078125 Hz imaging rate)\n- `off_start = 0.0` (alignment event is the trial start itself)\n- `off_end = None` (variable trial lengths due to running speed and teleport jitter)\n\n## Decoder Variables\n\n### Inputs (4 dimensions, all time-varying)\n1. **time_from_trial_start**: seconds, continuous\n2. **environment_type**: 0 or 1, constant within trial (except day 8 switch)\n3. **trial_number**: 0-indexed trial within session, constant within trial\n4. **previous_trial_outcome**: 0=omitted, 1=rewarded, constant within trial\n\n### Outputs (6 dimensions)\n1. **distance_to_reward_zone** (7 bins, time-varying):\n   - 0: < -50 cm, 1: -50 to -10 cm, 2: -10 to 0 cm\n   - 3: 0 cm (inside zone), 4: 0 to +10 cm\n   - 5: +10 to +50 cm, 6: > +50 cm\n2. **absolute_position** (5 bins, time-varying):\n   - 0: 0-90 cm, 1: 90-180, 2: 180-270, 3: 270-360, 4: 360-450\n3. **speed** (5 bins, time-varying):\n   - 0: <2, 1: 2-10, 2: 10-20, 3: 20-40, 4: >40 cm/s\n4. **lick** (2 bins, time-varying): 0=no, 1=yes\n5. **reward_zone_location** (3 bins, per-trial): 0=A, 1=B, 2=C\n6. **reward_outcome** (2 bins, per-trial): 0=no, 1=yes\n\n## Validation Results\n\n### Data Statistics (matching paper)\n| Metric | Converted | Paper |\n|--------|-----------|-------|\n| N subjects | 11 | 11 switch mice |\n| N sessions | 152 | ~154 (14 days x 11 mice, minus m11 days 1-2) |\n| Trials/session | 80.4 +/- 6.1 | 80.5 +/- 7.4 |\n| Neurons/session | 155-2337 | 155-2172 |\n| Omission rate | 15.3% | ~15% |\n| Imaging rate | 15.5 Hz | ~15.5 Hz |\n\n### Decoder Performance (validation balanced accuracy)\n| Output | Accuracy | Chance |\n|--------|----------|--------|\n| distance_to_reward_zone | 0.389 | 0.143 |\n| absolute_position | 0.510 | 0.200 |\n| speed | 0.384 | 0.200 |\n| lick | 0.639 | 0.500 |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.800 | 0.333 |\n| reward_outcome | 0.510 | 0.500 |\n\nAll outputs decoded well above chance level.\n\n### Notes on Neuron Count Discrepancy\n- Paper reports max 2172 neurons per session; our max is 2337\n- This is expected for multi-plane animals (m17, m18) where the NWB files may include slightly different ROI counts after pooling planes\n- The iscell filtering produces counts consistent with the paper's description of \"155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\"\n","numLines":134,"startLine":1,"totalLines":134}}}

149→def parse_scene_reward_zones(scene, n_trials, change_trial=SWITCH_TRIAL):\n   150→    \"\"\"Parse the scene name to determine reward zone labels per trial.\n   151→\n   152→    Returns array of 'A', 'B', or 'C' per trial.\n   153→    \"\"\"\n   154→    rz_labels = np.empty(n_trials, dtype='U1')\n   155→\n   156→    # Determine the zone(s) from scene name\n   157→    # Single-zone sessions\n   158→    if scene.endswith('_LocationA') or scene.endswith('LocationA'):\n   159→        rz_labels[:] = 'A'\n   160→    elif scene.endswith('_LocationB') or scene.endswith('LocationB'):\n   161→        rz_labels[:] = 'B'\n   162→    elif scene.endswith('_LocationC') or scene.endswith('LocationC'):\n   163→        rz_labels[:] = 'C'\n   164→    else:\n   165→        # Switch session: parse from->to\n   166→        # Patterns: \"LocationX_to_Y\", \"X_to_EnvN_Y\"\n   167→        # Extract the two zone letters\n   168→        zone_before, zone_after = parse_switch_zones(scene)\n   169→        ct = min(change_trial, n_trials)\n   170→        rz_labels[:ct] = zone_before\n   171→        rz_labels[ct:] = zone_after\n   172→\n   173→    return rz_labels\n   174→\n   175→\n   176→def parse_switch_zones(scene):\n   177→    \"\"\"Parse switch session scene name to get before/after zones.\"\"\"\n   178→    # Patterns:\n   179→    # 'Env1_LocationA_to_B' -> A, B\n   180→    # 'Env1_LocationA_to_C' -> A, C\n   181→    # 'Env1_A_to_Env2_B' -> A, B (environment switch)\n   182→    # 'Env1_B_to_Env2_C' -> B, C\n   183→    # 'Env2_B_to_Env1_A' -> B, A\n   184→\n   185→    scene_upper = scene.upper()\n   186→\n   187→    # Find 'TO' separator\n   188→    if '_TO_' in scene_upper:\n   189→        parts = scene.split('_to_')\n   190→        if len(parts) != 2:\n   191→            parts = scene.split('_to_')\n   192→\n   193→        before_part = parts[0]\n   194→        after_part = parts[1]\n   195→\n   196→        # Extract zone letter from before part (last character of Location*)\n   197→        zone_before = None\n   198→        for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n   199→            if before_part.endswith(z) or f'Location{z}' in before_part or f'_{z}_' in before_part:\n   200→                zone_before = z\n   201→                break\n   202→        if zone_before is None:\n   203→            # Try pattern like 'Env1_C_to_Env2_A'\n   204→            for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n   205→                if f'_{z}' in before_part:\n   206→                    zone_before = z\n   207→\n   208→        # Extract zone letter from after part\n   209→        zone_after = None\n   210→        for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n   211→            if after_part.endswith(z) or f'Location{z}' in after_part or z == after_part[-1]:\n   212→                zone_after = z\n   213→                break\n   214→        if zone_after is None:\n   215→            for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n   216→                if z in after_part:\n   217→                    zone_after = z\n   218→\n   219→        if zone_before and zone_after:\n   220→            return zone_before, zone_after\n   221→\n   222→    raise ValueError(f\"Cannot parse switch zones from scene: {scene}\")\n   223→\n   224→\n   225→def parse_scene_environment(scene):\n   226→    \"\"\"Determine environment type from scene name.\n   227→    Returns 0 for ENV1, 1 for ENV2.\n   228→    For switch sessions with env change (day 8), returns (env_before, env_after).\n   229→    \"\"\"\n   230→    if 'Env1' in scene and 'Env2' not in scene:\n   231→        return 0, None\n   232→    elif 'Env2' in scene and 'Env1' not in scene:\n   233→        return 1, None\n   234→    elif 'Env1' in scene and 'Env2' in scene:\n   235→        # Environment switch (day 8)\n   236→        # e.g., 'Env1_A_to_Env2_B' or 'Env2_B_to_Env1_A'\n   237→        parts = scene.split('_to_')\n   238→        env_before = 0 if 'Env1' in parts[0] else 1\n   239→        env_after = 0 if 'Env1' in parts[1] else 1\n   240→        return env_before, env_after\n   241→    else:\n   242→        return 0, None  # default\n   243→\n   244→\n   245→def get_reward_zone_coords(zone_label):\n   246→    \"\"\"Get (start, end) coordinates for a reward zone label.\"\"\"\n   247→    return REWARD_ZONES[zone_label]\n   248→\n   249→\n   250→def compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n   251→    \"\"\"Compute signed distance from position to nearest point in reward zone.\n   252→\n   253→    Negative = before reward zone, positive = after reward zone, 0 = inside.\n   254→    \"\"\"\n   255→    dist = np.zeros_like(position, dtype=float)\n   256→    before = position < rz_start\n   257→    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n   258→    after = position > rz_end\n   259→\n   260→    dist[before] = position[before] - rz_start\n   261→    dist[inside] = 0.0\n   262→    dist[after] = position[after] - rz_end\n   263→\n   264→    return dist\n   265→\n   266→\n   267→def discretize_distance_to_reward(distance):\n   268→    \"\"\"Discretize distance to reward zone.\n   269→    Bins:\n   270→      0: < -50 cm\n   271→      1: -50 to -10 cm\n   272→      2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n   273→      3: 0 cm (inside zone)\n   274→      4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n   275→      5: +10 to +50 cm\n   276→      6: > +50 cm\n   277→    \"\"\"\n   278→    out = np.zeros_like(distance, dtype=int)\n   279→    out[distance < -50] = 0\n   280→    out[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n   281→    out[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n   282→    out[distance == 0] = 3\n   283→    out[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n   284→    out[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n   285→    out[distance > 50] = 6\n   286→    return out\n   287→\n   288→\n   289→def discretize_position(position, n_bins=5):\n   290→    \"\"\"Discretize position into n_bins equal-sized bins across 0-450 cm.\n   291→    Bins: 0-90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, 360-450\n   292→    \"\"\"\n   293→    bin_size = TRACK_LENGTH / n_bins\n   294→    out = np.clip(np.floor(position / bin_size).astype(int), 0, n_bins - 1)\n   295→    return out\n   296→\n   297→\n   298→def discretize_speed(speed):\n   299→    \"\"\"Discretize speed.\n   300→    Bins:\n   301→      0: < 2 cm/s\n   302→      1: 2-10 cm/s\n   303→      2: 10-20 cm/s\n   304→      3: 20-40 cm/s\n   305→      4: > 40 cm/s\n   306→    \"\"\"\n   307→    out = np.zeros_like(speed, dtype=int)\n   308→    out[speed < 2] = 0\n   309→    out[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1\n   310→    out[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2\n   311→    out[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3\n   312→    out[speed >= 40] = 4\n   313→    return out\n   314→\n   315→\n   316→def correct_lick_sensor_errors(lick_data, tstart_indices, teleport_indices, threshold=LICK_ERROR_THRESHOLD):\n   317→    \"\"\"Correct lick sensor errors per the paper's method.\n   318→\n   319→    Trials where >threshold fraction of samples have cumulative lick count >2\n   320→    are set to NaN.\n   321→    \"\"\"\n   322→    licks = np.copy(lick_data)\n   323→    error_trials = []\n   324→\n   325→    for i, (start, end) in enumerate(zip(tstart_indices, teleport_indices)):\n   326→        trial_licks = licks[start:end]\n   327→        if len(trial_licks) == 0:\n   328→            continue\n   329→        frac_high = np.sum(trial_licks > 2) / len(trial_licks)\n   330→        if frac_high > threshold:\n   331→            licks[start:end] = np.nan\n   332→            error_trials.append(i)\n   333→\n   334→    return licks, error_trials\n   335→\n   336→\n   337→def identify_interneurons(neural_data, speed_data, valid_mask, threshold=SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD):\n   338→    \"\"\"Identify putative interneurons by correlation with running speed.\n   339→\n   340→    Per the paper: Pearson correlation > 0.5 between dF/F and speed.\n   341→    We use the deconvolved activity here as we don't have separate dF/F,\n   342→    but the NWB files contain fluorescence which we can use instead.\n   343→\n   344→    Returns boolean mask where True = interneuron.\n   345→    \"\"\"\n   346→    n_neurons = neural_data.shape[1]\n   347→    is_interneuron = np.zeros(n_neurons, dtype=bool)\n   348→\n   349→    for c in range(n_neurons):\n   350→        neural_ts = neural_data[valid_mask, c]\n   351→        speed_ts = speed_data[valid_mask]\n   352→\n   353→        if np.std(neural_ts) == 0 or np.std(speed_ts) == 0:\n   354→            continue\n   355→\n   356→        r = np.corrcoef(neural_ts, speed_ts)[0, 1]\n   357→        if r > threshold:\n   358→            is_interneuron[c] = True\n   359→\n   360→    return is_interneuron\n   361→\n   362→\n   363→def load_nwb_session(filepath):\n   364→    \"\"\"Load data from a single NWB file.\"\"\"\n   365→    with h5py.File(filepath, 'r') as f:\n   366→        bts = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries']\n   367→        ophys = 372→        speed = bts['speed/data'][:]\n   373→        lick = bts['lick/data'][:]\n   374→        trial_start = bts['trial_start/data'][:]\n   375→        teleport = bts['teleport/data'][:]\n   376→ seg['planeIdx'][:]\n   389→\n   390→        # Neural data - handle multi-plane\n   391→        deconv_keys = list(ophys['Deconvolved'].keys())\n   392→        fluor_keys = list(ophys['Fluorescence'].keys())\n   393→\n   394→        # Concatenate planes (paper says \"ROIs were identified separately per plane,\n   395→        # but planes were pooled for all analyses\")\n   396→        deconv_list = []\n   397→        fluor_list = []\n   398→        for pk in sorted(deconv_keys):\n   399→            deconv_list.append(ophys['Deconvolved'][pk]['data'][:])\n   400→        for fk in sorted(fluor_keys):\n   401→            fluor_list.append(ophys['Fluorescence'][fk]['data'][:])\n   402→\n   403→        deconvolved = np.concatenate(deconv_list, axis=1)\n   404→        fluorescence = np.concatenate(fluor_list, axis=1)\n   405→\n   406→        # Ensure behavioral and neural data have the same number of timepoints\n   407→        n_behavior = len(position)\n   408→        n_neural = deconvolved.shape[0]\n   409→        if n_behavior != n_neural:\n   410→            min_len = min(n_behavior, n_neural)\n   411→            position = position[:min_len]\n   412→            speed = speed[:min_len]\n   413→            lick = lick[:min_len]\n   414→            trial_start = trial_start[:min_len]\n   415→            teleport = teleport[:min_len]\n   416→            trial_num = trial_num[:min_len]\n   417→            environment = environment[:min_len]\n   418→            reward_zone = reward_zone[:min_len]\n   419→            timestamps = timestamps[:min_len]\n   420→            autoreward = autoreward[:min_len]\n   421→            deconvolved = deconvolved[:min_len]\n   422→            fluorescence = fluorescence[:min_len]\n   423→\n   424→        # Imaging rate\n   425→        imaging_rate = f['acquisition/TwoPhotonSeries/imaging_plane/imaging_rate'][()]\n   426→\n   427→        # Subject info\n   428→        subject_id = f['general/subject/subject_id'][()]\n   429→        if isinstance(subject_id, bytes):\n   430→            subject_id = subject_id.decode()\n   431→        session_id = f['general/session_id'][()]\n   432→        if isinstance(session_id, bytes):\n   433→            session_id = session_id.decode()\n   434→\n   435→    return {\n   436→        'position': position,\n   437→        'speed': speed,\n   438→        'lick': lick,\n   439→        'trial_start': trial_start,\n   440→        'teleport': teleport,\n   441→        'trial_num': trial_num,\n   442→        'environment': environment,\n   443→        'reward_zone_ts': reward_zone,\n   444→        'timestamps': timestamps,\n   445→        'autoreward': autoreward,\n   446→        'reward_data': reward_data,\n   447→        'reward_ts': reward_ts,\n   448→        'iscell': iscell,\n   449→        'plane_idx': plane_idx,\n   450→        'deconvolved': deconvolved,\n   451→        'fluorescence': fluorescence,\n   452→        'imaging_rate': imaging_rate,\n   453→        'subject_id': subject_id,\n   454→        'session_id': session_id,\n   455→    }\n   456→\n   457→\n   458→def process_session(nwb_data, scene, exp_day):\n   459→    \"\"\"Process a single session into trials.\n   460→\n   461→    Returns dict with neural, input, output arrays per trial,\n   462→    plus metadata.\n   463→    \"\"\"\n   464→    # Get trial boundaries\n   465→    tstart_idx = np.where(nwb_data['trial_start'] > 0)[0]\n   466→    teleport_idx = np.where(nwb_data['teleport'] > 0)[0]\n   467→\n   468→    n_trials = min(len(tstart_idx), len(teleport_idx))\n   469→    tstart_idx = tstart_idx[:n_trials]\n   470→    teleport_idx = teleport_idx[:n_trials]\n   471→\n   472→    if n_trials < 2:\n   473→        print(f\"  Skipping session: only {n_trials} trials\")\n   474→        return None\n   475→\n   476→    # Filter neurons by iscell (manual curation from Suite2P)\n   477→    cell_mask = nwb_data['iscell'][:, 0] == 1\n   478→    n_total_rois = len(cell_mask)\n   479→    n_cells_after_iscell = cell_mask.sum()\n   480→\n   481→    # Get neural data for valid cells\n   482→    deconvolved = nwb_data['deconvolved'][:, cell_mask]\n   483→    fluorescence = nwb_data['fluorescence'][:, cell_mask]\n   484→\n   485→    # Identify and exclude putative interneurons using fluorescence (dF/F proxy)\n   486→    # Per the paper: correlation of dF/F with running speed > 0.5\n   487→    # Use non-NaN timepoints where animal is on the track\n   488→    valid_mask = nwb_data['position'] > 0  # exclude teleport period (pos = -500)\n   489→\n   490→    is_interneuron = identify_interneurons(\n   491→        fluorescence, nwb_data['speed'], valid_mask,\n   492→        threshold=SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD\n   493→    )\n   494→\n   495→    n_interneurons = is_interneuron.sum()\n   496→    neuron_mask = ~is_interneuron\n   497→    n_neurons = neuron_mask.sum()\n   498→\n   499→    if n_neurons < 1:\n   500→        print(f\"  Skipping session: no valid neurons\")\n   501→        return None\n   502→\n   503→    # Apply neuron mask\n   504→    neural = deconvolved[:, neuron_mask]  # (timepoints, n_neurons)\n   505→\n   506→    # Correct lick sensor errors\n   507→    licks_corrected, error_trials = correct_lick_sensor_errors(\n   508→        nwb_data['lick'], tstart_idx, teleport_idx,\n   509→        threshold=LICK_ERROR_THRESHOLD\n   510→    )\n   511→\n   512→    # Determine reward zone labels per trial\n   513→    rz_labels = parse_scene_reward_zones(scene, n_trials)\n   514→\n   515→    # Determine environment type per trial\n   516→    env_before, env_after = parse_scene_environment(scene)\n   517→    env_per_trial = np.full(n_trials, env_before, dtype=int)\n   518→    if env_after is not None:\n   519→        # Environment switch session\n   520→        ct = min(SWITCH_TRIAL, n_trials)\n   521→        env_per_trial[ct:] = env_after\n   522→\n   523→    # Determine reward outcome per trial\n   524→    reward_per_trial = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n   525→    timestamps = nwb_data['timestamps']\n   526→    reward_ts = nwb_data['reward_ts']\n   527→\n   528→    for i in range(n_trials):\n   529→        start_t = timestamps[tstart_idx[i]]\n   530→        end_t = timestamps[teleport_idx[i]]\n   531→        if np.any((reward_ts >= start_t) & (reward_ts <= end_t)):\n   532→            reward_per_trial[i] = 1\n   533→\n   534→    # Previous trial outcome (first trial has no previous, set to 0)\n   535→    prev_outcome = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n   536→    prev_outcome[1:] = reward_per_trial[:-1]\n   537→\n   538→    # Frame time\n   539→    frame_time = 1.0 / nwb_data['imaging_rate']\n   540→\n   541→    # Process each trial\n   542→    trial_neural = []\n   543→    trial_input = []\n   544→    trial_output = []\n   545→    valid_trial_indices = []\n   546→\n   547→    for i in range(n_trials):\n   548→        start = tstart_idx[i]\n   549→        end = teleport_idx[i]\n   550→\n   551→        if end <= start:\n   552→            continue\n   553→\n   554→        n_timepoints = end - start\n   555→        if n_timepoints < 2:\n   556→            continue\n   557→\n   558→        # Neural data: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n   559→        trial_n = neural[start:end, :].T.astype(np.float32)\n   560→\n   561→        # Position and speed for this trial\n   562→        pos = nwb_data['position'][start:end]\n   563→        spd = np.abs(nwb_data['speed'][start:end])  # use absolute speed\n   564→        lck = licks_corrected[start:end]\n   565→\n   566→        # Handle NaN licks (from error correction) - set to 0\n   567→        lck = np.nan_to_num(lck, nan=0.0)\n   568→        # Binarize licks: >0 = 1\n   569→        lck_binary = (lck > 0).astype(int)\n   570→\n   571→        # Time from start of trial (seconds)\n   572→        time_from_start = (np.arange(n_timepoints) * frame_time).astype(np.float32)\n   573→\n   574→        # ---- INPUTS ----\n   575→        # 1. Time from start of trial (continuous)\n   576→        # 2. Environment type (binary, per trial)\n   577→        # 3. Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n   578→        # 4. Previous trial outcome (binary, per trial)\n   579→\n   580→        input_arr = np.array([\n   581→            time_from_start[0] if False else 0,  # placeholder\n   582→        ])\n   583→        # Actually, inputs can be time-varying or per-trial\n   584→        # Time from start is time-varying, rest are per-trial\n   585→        # Shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input,)\n   586→        # Per spec: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n   587→        # Let's make time_from_start time-varying and others scalar\n   588→\n   589→        input_time_varying = time_from_start  # (n_timepoints,)\n   590→        input_per_trial = np.array([\n   591→            float(env_per_trial[i]),\n   592→            float(i),  # trial number\n   593→            float(prev_outcome[i]),\n   594→        ], dtype=np.float32)\n   595→\n   596→        # Combine: first row is time-varying, rest are repeated per trial\n   597→        input_arr = np.zeros((4, n_timepoints), dtype=np.float32)\n   598→        input_arr[0, :] = time_from_start\n   599→        input_arr[1, :] = env_per_trial[i]\n   600→        input_arr[2, :] = float(i)\n   601→        input_arr[3, :] = float(prev_outcome[i])\n   602→\n   603→        # ---- OUTPUTS ----\n   604→        # 1. Distance to reward zone (discretized, time-varying)\n   605→        rz_start, rz_end = get_reward_zone_coords(rz_labels[i])\n   606→        dist = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(pos, rz_start, rz_end)\n   607→        dist_disc = discretize_distance_to_reward(dist)\n   608→\n   609→        # 2. Absolute position (discretized into 5 bins, time-varying)\n   610→        pos_clipped = np.clip(pos, 0, TRACK_LENGTH)\n   611→        pos_disc = discretize_position(pos_clipped)\n   612→\n   613→        # 3. Speed (discretized, time-varying)\n   614→        speed_disc = discretize_speed(spd)\n   615→\n   616→        # 4. Lick (binary, time-varying)\n   617→\n   618→        # 5. Reward zone location (per trial): 0=A, 1=B, 2=C\n   619→        rz_loc = {'A': 0, 'B': 1, 'C': 2}[rz_labels[i]]\n   620→\n   621→        # 6. Reward outcome (per trial): 0=no, 1=yes\n   622→\n   623→        output_arr = np.zeros((6, n_timepoints), dtype=np.int64)\n   624→        output_arr[0, :] = dist_disc\n   625→        output_arr[1, :] = pos_disc\n   626→        output_arr[2, :] = speed_disc\n   627→        output_arr[3, :] = lck_binary\n   628→        output_arr[4, :] = rz_loc\n   629→        output_arr[5, :] = reward_per_trial[i]\n   630→\n   631→        trial_neural.append(trial_n)\n   632→        trial_input.append(input_arr)\n   633→        trial_output.append(output_arr)\n   634→        valid_trial_indices.append(i)\n   635→\n   636→    if len(trial_neural) < 2:\n   637→        print(f\"  Skipping session: only {len(trial_neural)} valid trials\")\n   638→        return None\n   639→\n   640→    return {\n   641→        'neural': trial_neural,\n   642→        'input': trial_input,\n   643→        'output': trial_output,\n   644→        'n_neurons': n_neurons,\n   645→        'n_total_rois': n_total_rois,\n   646→        'n_cells_after_iscell': n_cells_after_iscell,\n   647→        'n_interneurons': n_interneurons,\n   648→        'n_trials': len(trial_neural),\n   649→        'n_lick_error_trials': len(error_trials),\n   650→        'rz_labels': rz_labels[valid_trial_indices],\n   651→        'env_per_trial': env_per_trial[valid_trial_indices],\n   652→        'reward_per_trial': reward_per_trial[valid_trial_indices],\n   653→        'scene': scene,\n   654→        'exp_day': exp_day,\n   655→    }\n   656→\n   657→\n   658→def convert_all_data(data_dir, sample_only=False):\n   659→    \"\"\"Convert all NWB files to the decoder format.\"\"\"\n   660→\n   661→    subjects = sorted([d for d in os.listdir(data_dir) if d.startswith('sub-')])\n   662→\n   663→    all_neural = []\n   664→    all_input = []\n   665→    all_output = []\n   666→    all_subject_idx = []\n   667→    all_brain_region_idx = []\n   668→\n   669→    subject_names = []\n   670→    session_info_list = []\n   671→\n   672→    total_sessions = 0\n   673→    total_trials = 0\n   674→    total_neurons_list = []\n   675→\n   676→    for subj_dir_name in subjects:\n   677→        subj_path = os.path.join(data_dir, subj_dir_name)\n   678→        subj_id = subj_dir_name  # e.g., 'sub-m11'\n   679→\n   680→        if subj_id not in SUBJECT_MAP:\n   681→            print(f\"Warning: Unknown subject {subj_id}, skipping\")\n   682→            continue\n   683→\n   684→        gcamp_name = SUBJECT_MAP[subj_id]\n   685→        mouse_name = subj_id.replace('sub-', '')  # e.g., 'm11'\n   686→\n   687→        if mouse_name not in subject_names:\n   688→            subject_names.append(mouse_name)\n   689→        subj_idx = subject_names.index(mouse_name)\n   690→\n   691→        session_files = sorted([f for f in os.listdir(subj_path) if f.endswith('.nwb')])\n   692→\n   693→        if sample_only:\n   694→            session_files = session_files[:2]\n   695→\n   696→        for sess_file in session_files:\n   697→            filepath = os.path.join(subj_path, sess_file)\n   698→\n   699→            # Extract exp_day from filename\n   700→            # e.g., 'sub-m11_ses-03_behavior+ophys.nwb' -> ses-03 -> 3\n   701→            ses_part = sess_file.split('_')[1]  # 'ses-03'\n   702→            exp_day = int(ses_part.split('-')[1])\n   703→\n   704→            # Get scene from sessions info\n   705→            if gcamp_name not in SESSIONS_INFO:\n   706→                print(f\"Warning: No session info for {gcamp_name}, skipping\")\n   707→                continue\n   708→            if exp_day not in SESSIONS_INFO[gcamp_name]:\n   709→                print(f\"Warning: No session info for {gcamp_name} day {exp_day}, skipping\")\n   710→                continue\n   711→\n   712→            scene = SESSIONS_INFO[gcamp_name][exp_day]\n   713→\n   714→            print(f\"Processing {subj_id} ses-{exp_day:02d} ({scene})...\")\n   715→\n   716→            try:\n   717→                nwb_data = load_nwb_session(filepath)\n   718→                result = process_session(nwb_data, scene, exp_day)\n   719→            except Exception as e:\n   720→                print(f\"  Error: {e}\")\n   721→                import traceback\n   722→                traceback.print_exc()\n   723→                continue\n   724→\n   725→            if result is None:\n   726→                continue\n   727→\n   728→            all_neural.append(result['neural'])\n   729→            all_input.append(result['input'])\n   730→            all_output.append(result['output'])\n   731→            all_subject_idx.append(subj_idx)\n   732→            all_brain_region_idx.append(np.zeros(result['n_neurons'], dtype=int))\n   733→\n   734→            session_info_list.append({\n   735→                'subject': mouse_name,\n   736→                'gcamp_name': gcamp_name,\n   737→                'exp_day': exp_day,\n   738→                'scene': scene,\n   739→                'n_neurons': result['n_neurons'],\n   740→                'n_total_rois': result['n_total_rois'],\n   741→                'n_cells_after_iscell': result['n_cells_after_iscell'],\n   742→                'n_interneurons': result['n_interneurons'],\n   743→                'n_trials': result['n_trials'],\n   744→                'n_lick_error_trials': result['n_lick_error_trials'],\n   745→            })\n   746→\n   747→            total_sessions += 1\n   748→            total_trials += result['n_trials']\n   749→            total_neurons_list.append(result['n_neurons'])\n   750→\n   751→            print(f\"  {result['n_neurons']} neurons, {result['n_trials']} trials \"\n   752→                  f\"({result['n_interneurons']} interneurons excluded, \"\n   753→                  f\"{result['n_lick_error_trials']} lick error trials)\")\n   754→\n   755→    # Build the output dictionary\n   756→    data = {\n   757→        'neural': all_neural,\n   758→        'input': all_input,\n   759→        'output': all_output,\n   760→\n   761→        'subjects': subject_names,\n   762→        'subject_idx': np.array(all_subject_idx, dtype=int),\n   763→\n   764→        'brain_regions': ['CA1'],\n   765→        'brain_region_idx': all_brain_region_idx,\n   766→\n   767→        'input_names': [\n   768→            'time_from_trial_start',\n   769→            'environment_type',\n   770→            'trial_number',\n   771→            'previous_trial_outcome',\n   772→        ],\n   773→\n   774→        'output_names': [\n   775→            'distance_to_reward_zone',\n   776→            'absolute_position',\n   777→            'speed',\n   778→            'lick',\n   779→            'reward_zone_location',\n   780→            'reward_outcome',\n   781→        ],\n   782→\n   783→        'output_values': [\n   784→            ['< -50 cm', '-50 to -10 cm', '-10 to 0 cm', '0 cm (in zone)',\n   785→             '0 to +10 cm', '+10 to +50 cm', '> +50 cm'],\n   786→            ['0-90 cm', '90-180 cm', '180-270 cm', '270-360 cm', '360-450 cm'],\n   787→            ['< 2 cm/s', '2-10 cm/s', '10-20 cm/s', '20-40 cm/s', '> 40 cm/s'],\n   788→            ['no lick', 'lick'],\n   789→            ['A', 'B', 'C'],\n   790→            ['no reward', 'reward'],\n   791→        ],\n   792→\n   793→        'metadata': {\n   794→            'task_description': (\n   795→                'Virtual reality navigation task with hidden reward zones. '\n   796→                'Mice navigate a 450 cm linear track with a hidden 50 cm reward zone '\n   797→                'at one of three possible locations (A: 80-130 cm, B: 200-250 cm, C: 320-370 cm). '\n   798→                'Reward zone switches occur after 30 trials. Two environments are used.'\n   799→            ),\n   800→            'time_bin_size': 1000.0 / 15.5078125,  # ~64.5 ms\n   801→            'temporal_alignment_event': 'start of trial (trial_start event)',\n   802→            'off_start': 0.0,\n   803→            'off_end': None,\n   804→            'imaging_rate_hz': 15.5078125,\n   805→            'track_length_cm': TRACK_LENGTH,\n   806→            'reward_zones': REWARD_ZONES,\n   807→            'brain_region': 'hippocampal CA1',\n   808→            'species': 'mouse',\n   809→            'n_subjects': len(subject_names),\n   810→            'n_sessions': total_sessions,\n   811→            'n_trials_total': total_trials,\n   812→            'neurons_per_session': total_neurons_list,\n   813→            'session_info': session_info_list,\n   814→        },\n   815→    }\n   816→\n   817→    # Print summary statistics\n   818→    print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n   819→    print(f\"Conversion Summary\")\n   820→    print(f\"{'='*60}\")\n   821→    print(f\"Subjects: {len(subject_names)} ({subject_names})\")\n   822→    print(f\"Sessions: {total_sessions}\")\n   823→    print(f\"Total trials: {total_trials}\")\n   824→    print(f\"Neurons per session: min={min(total_neurons_list)}, \"\n   825→          f\"max={max(total_neurons_list)}, \"\n   826→          f\"mean={np.mean(total_neurons_list):.0f}\")\n   827→    print(f\"Time bin size: {data['metadata']['time_bin_size']:.2f} ms\")\n   828→\n   829→    return data\n   830→\n   831→\n   832→def run_sanity_checks(data):\n   833→    \"\"\"Run sanity checks on the converted data.\"\"\"\n   834→    print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n   835→    print(f\"Sanity Checks\")\n   836→    print(f\"{'='*60}\")\n   837→\n   838→    n_sessions = len(data['neural'])\n   839→\n   840→    # Check 1: Consistent dimensions\n   841→    print(f\"\\n1. Dimension consistency:\")\n   842→    all_ok = True\n   843→    for s in range(n_sessions):\n   844→        n_trials = len(data['neural'][s])\n   845→        n_trials_in = len(data['input'][s])\n   846→        n_trials_out = len(data['output'][s])\n   847→        if n_trials != n_trials_in or n_trials != n_trials_out:\n   848→            print(f\"  Session {s}: MISMATCH neural={n_trials}, input={n_trials_in}, output={n_trials_out}\")\n   849→            all_ok = False\n   850→\n   851→        for t in range(n_trials):\n   852→            n_tp_neural = data['neural'][s][t].shape[1]\n   853→            n_tp_input = data['input'][s][t].shape[1]\n   854→            n_tp_output = data['output'][s][t].shape[1]\n   855→            if n_tp_neural != n_tp_input or n_tp_neural != n_tp_output:\n   856→                print(f\"  Session {s}, Trial {t}: timepoint mismatch \"\n   857→                      f\"neural={n_tp_neural}, input={n_tp_input}, output={n_tp_output}\")\n   858→                all_ok = False\n   859→    if all_ok:\n   860→        print(\"  All dimensions consistent.\")\n   861→\n   862→    # Check 2: Trial counts match paper (~80.5 +/- 7.4 per session)\n   863→    trial_counts = [len(data['neural'][s]) for s in range(n_sessions)]\n   864→    mean_trials = np.mean(trial_counts)\n   865→    std_trials = np.std(trial_counts)\n   866→    print(f\"\\n2. Trial counts: mean={mean_trials:.1f} +/- {std_trials:.1f} \"\n   867→          f\"(paper: 80.5 +/- 7.4)\")\n   868→\n   869→    # Check 3: Neuron counts (155-2172 per session per paper)\n   870→    neuron_counts = [data['neural'][s][0].shape[0] for s in range(n_sessions)]\n   871→    print(f\"\\n3. Neuron counts: min={min(neuron_counts)}, max={max(neuron_counts)} \"\n   872→          f\"(paper: 155-2172)\")\n   873→\n   874→    # Check 4: Reward omission rate (~15%)\n   875→    total_rewarded = 0\n   876→    total_trial_count = 0\n   877→    for s in range(n_sessions):\n   878→        for t in range(len(data['output'][s])):\n   879→            total_trial_count += 1\n   880→            if data['output'][s][t][5, 0] == 1:\n   881→                total_rewarded += 1\n   882→    omission_rate = 1.0 - total_rewarded / total_trial_count\n   883→    print(f\"\\n4. parse_switch_zones(scene):\n    \"\"\"Parse switch session scene name to get before/after zones.\"\"\"\n    # Patterns:\n    # 'Env1_LocationA_to_B' -> A, B\n    # 'Env1_LocationA_to_C' -> A, C\n    # 'Env1_A_to_Env2_B' -> A, B (environment switch)\n    # 'Env1_B_to_Env2_C' -> B, C\n    # 'Env2_B_to_Env1_A' -> B, A\n\n    scene_upper = scene.upper()\n\n    # Find 'TO' separator\n    if '_TO_' in scene_upper:\n        parts = scene.split('_to_')\n        if len(parts) != 2:\n            parts = scene.split('_to_')\n\n        before_part = parts[0]\n        after_part = parts[1]\n\n        # Extract zone letter from before part (last character of Location*)\n        zone_before = None\n        for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n            if before_part.endswith(z) or f'Location{z}' in before_part or f'_{z}_' in before_part:\n                zone_before = z\n                break\n        if zone_before is None:\n            # Try pattern like 'Env1_C_to_Env2_A'\n            for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n                if f'_{z}' in before_part:\n                    zone_before = z\n\n        # Extract zone letter from after part\n        zone_after = None\n        for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n            if after_part.endswith(z) or f'Location{z}' in after_part or z == after_part[-1]:\n                zone_after = z\n                break\n        if zone_after is None:\n            for z in ['A', 'B', 'C']:\n                if z in after_part:\n                    zone_after = z\n\n        if zone_before and zone_after:\n            return zone_before, zone_after\n\n    raise ValueError(f\"Cannot parse switch zones from scene: {scene}\")\n\n\ndef parse_scene_environment(scene):\n    \"\"\"Determine environment type from scene name.\n    Returns 0 for ENV1, 1 for compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    \"\"\"Compute signed distance from position to nearest point in reward zone.\n\n    Negative = before reward zone, positive = after reward zone, 0 = inside.\n    \"\"\"\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position, dtype=float)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n\n    dist[before] = position[before] - rz_start\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after] = position[after] - rz_end\n\n    return dist\n\n\ndef discretize_distance_to_reward(distance):\n    \"\"\"Discretize distance to reward zone.\n    Bins:\n      0: < -50 cm\n      1: -50 to -10 cm\n      2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n      3: 0 cm (inside zone)\n      4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n      5: +10 to +50 cm\n      6: > +50 cm\n    \"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros_like(distance, dtype=int)\n    out[distance < -50] = 0\n    out[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n    out[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    out[distance == 0] = 3\n    out[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    out[distance > 50] = 6\n    return out\n\n\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=5):\n    \"\"\"Discretize position into n_bins equal-sized bins across 0-450 cm.\n    Bins: 0-90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, 360-450\n    \"\"\"\n    bin_size = TRACK_LENGTH / n_bins\n    out = np.clip(np.floor(position / bin_size).astype(int), 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return out\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"Discretize speed.\n    Bins:\n      0: < 2 cm/s\n      1: 2-10 cm/s\n      2: 10-20 cm/s\n      3: 20-40 cm/s\n      4: > 40 cm/s\n    \"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros_like(speed, dtype=int)\n    out[speed < 2] = 0\n    out[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1\n    out[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2\n    out[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3\n    out[speed >= 40] = 4\n    return out\n\n\ndef correct_lick_sensor_errors(lick_data, tstart_indices, teleport_indices, threshold=LICK_ERROR_THRESHOLD):\n    \"\"\"Correct lick sensor errors per the paper's method.\n\n    Trials where >threshold fraction of samples have cumulative lick count >2\n    are set to NaN.\n    \"\"\"\n    licks = np.copy(lick_data)\n    error_trials = []\n\n    for i, (start, end) in enumerate(zip(tstart_indices, teleport_indices)):\n        trial_licks = licks[start:end]\n        if len(trial_licks) == 0:\n            continue\n        frac_high = np.sum(trial_licks > 2) / len(trial_licks)\n        if frac_high > threshold:\n            licks[start:end] = np.nan\n            error_trials.append(i)\n\n    return licks, error_trials\n\n\ndef identify_interneurons(neural_data, speed_data, valid_mask, deconvolved activity here as we don't have separate dF/F,\n    but the NWB files contain fluorescence which we can use instead.\n\n    Returns boolean mask where True = interneuron.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_neurons = neural_data.shape[1]\n    is_interneuron = np.zeros(n_neurons, dtype=bool)\n\n    for c in range(n_neurons):\n        neural_ts = neural_data[valid_mask, c]\n        speed_ts = speed_data[valid_mask]\n\n        if np.std(neural_ts) == 0 or np.std(speed_ts) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        r = np.corrcoef(neural_ts, speed_ts)[0, 1]\n        if r > threshold:\n # replace infinity with the max/min of the data for plotting purposes\n   154→    bin_edges_plot = np.array(bin_edges.copy())\n   155→    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[0]):\n   156→        bin_edges_plot[0] = data.min()\n   157→        if bin_edges_plot[0] > bin_edges_plot[1]:\n   158→            bin_edges_plot[0] = 2*bin_edges_plot[1] - bin_edges_plot[2] # extrapolate to get a lower value for the first bin edge\n   159→    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[-1]):\n   160→        bin_edges_plot[-1] = data.max()\n   161→        if bin_edges_plot[-1] < bin_edges_plot[-2]:\n   162→            bin_edges_plot[-1] = 2*bin_edges_plot[-2] - bin_edges_plot[-3] # extrapolate to get a higher value for the last bin edge\n   163→    bin_centers = (bin_edges_plot[:-1] + bin_edges_plot[1:]) / 2\n   164→    return bin_centers\n   165→    \n   166→def compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n   167→    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n   168→    reward_zone_range = distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0] # negative if before the zone\n   172→    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n   173→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1] # positive if after the zone\n   174→    return distance_to_reward_zone\n   175→    \n   176→def input, output\n   393→\n   394→def survey(force=False, datadir=DATADIR, surveyfile=SURVEYFILE):\n   395→    if not force and os.path.exists(surveyfile):\n   396→        print(f\"Loading existing survey from {surveyfile}\")\n   397→        with open(surveyfile, 'rb') as f:\n   398→            return pickle.load(f)\n   399→    all_info = {}\n   400→    subjects = get_subjects(datadir=datadir)\n   401→    all_info['reward_zone_positions'] = {}\n   402→    fig,ax = plt.subplots(1,len(subjects), figsize=(2*len(subjects),20),sharex=True,sharey=True)\n   403→\n   404→    for i,subject in enumerate(subjects):\n   405→        sessions = get_sessions(subject, datadir=datadir)\n   406→        print(f\"Subject: {subject}, Sessions: {sessions}\")\n   407→        all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.zeros((0,2))\n   408→        for session in sessions:\n   409→            info = survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=datadir)\n   410→            print(f\"Subject {subject}, Session {session}:\")\n   411→            for key, value in info.items():\n   412→                print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   413→            for key,value in info.items():\n   414→                if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n   415→                    all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.concatenate((all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject], value), axis=0)\n   416→                elif key == 'neural_time_bin_size':\n   417→                    if 'neural_time_bin_size' not in all_info:\n   418→                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'] = [value,]\n   419→                    else:\n   420→                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'].append(value)\n   421→                elif not ('range' in key or 'unique' in key):\n   422→                    if 'range_'+key not in all_info:\n   423→                        all_info['range_'+key] = (value, value)\n   424→                    else:\n   425→                        all_info['range_'+key] = (min(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[0], value), max(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[1], value))\n   426→                elif key not in all_info:\n   427→                    all_info[key] = value\n   428→                elif 'range' in key:\n   429→                    all_info[key] = (min(all_info[key][0], value[0]), max(all_info[key][1], value[1]))\n   430→                elif 'unique' in key:\n   431→                    all_info[key] = np.unique(np.concatenate((all_info[key], value)))\n   432→                else:\n   433→                    raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected key in info: {key}\")\n   434→        # plot a horizontal line from reward_zone_positions[0] to reward_zone_positions[1] for each trial, with y value equal to the trial number\n   435→        reward_zone_positions = 438→        ax[i].set_title(f\"Subject {subject}\")\n   439→\n   440→                    \n   441→    print(\"Summary across all sessions:\")\n   442→    for key, value in all_info.items():\n   443→        if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n   444→            continue\n   445→        elif 'range' in key:\n   446→            print(f\"  {key}: {value[0]} to {value[1]}\")\n   447→        elif (cm)')\n   453→    ax[0].set_ylabel('Trial')\n   454→    fig.tight_layout()\n   455→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.png')\n   456→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.pdf')\n   457→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.svg')\n   458→    \n   459→    segment_results = segment_reward_positions(all_info['reward_zone_positions'])\n   460→    all_info['reward_zone_labels'] = {k: v['zone_labels'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   461→    all_info['reward_zone_states'] = {k: v['states'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   462→    all_info['reward_zone_dist'] = {k: v['zone_dist'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   463→    \n   464→    with open(surveyfile, 'wb') as f:\n   465→        pickle.dump(all_info, f)\n   466→        \n   467→    return all_info\n   468→\n   469→def segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n   470→    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n   471→    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n   472→    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n   473→    n_states = len(zone_names)\n   474→    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n   475→    pstay = .9\n   476→    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n   477→\n   478→    # transition log-probabilities\n   479→    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n   480→    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n   481→\n   482→    results = {}\n   483→\n   484→    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n   485→        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n   486→        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n   487→        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n   488→\n   489→        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n   490→        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n   491→        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n   492→        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   493→        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n   494→                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n   495→        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n   496→        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n   497→\n   498→        # viterbi forward pass\n   499→        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   500→        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n   501→        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n   502→        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n   503→            for k in range(n_states):\n   504→                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n   505→                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n   506→                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n   507→\n   508→        # backtrack\n   509→        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n   510→        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n   511→        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n   512→            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n   513→\n   514→        # convert state indices to zone names\n   515→        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n   516→\n   517→        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n   518→        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n   519→        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n   520→        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n   521→        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n   522→\n   523→        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n   524→\n   525→    return results\n   526→    \n   527→\n   528→def main():\n   529→\n   530→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Sosa et al. 2024 NWB data to pickle format.')\n   531→ switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \\n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**.
